reason every group since the 2026-08-17 audit has been appended -- renumbering would break every rank this file
has ever been cited by -- but they differ from the eighteen in the one respect that paragraph is counting: each
cites a real source record written in the same session (`CORE-034`, `CORE-035` and `UI-037` respectively), not a
session name. The running total of entries with **no** source record therefore stays at **eighteen**, and the
assignment statement gains no new unassigned entries from this group. **Their severities, efforts and risks are
this session's judgment, not a measured value**, on the same terms as the 6b-iii and 6b-iv groups. What *was*
measured is recorded with its command in
`.dir-sdlc/sessions/2026-09-17-enum-zero-values/measurement.md` -- including the correction of an inherited
`14 of 16` to the measured `14 of 17`, which is the reason `MASTER-178` exists as a record rather than as a
line in a session report. Both of those figures describe the tree **as found at session start**; the same
census re-run on the post-change tree returns `12 of 17`, for the reason recorded in `CORE-035`.

**Why `MASTER-168`, `MASTER-169` and `MASTER-170` were filed rather than fixed.** All three came out of `MASTER-086`'s re-measurement as production findings wearing test clothing, and they were **deliberately not written as tests, per escalation E2** -- a decision, not an omission. `TEST-020`'s clause (d) asks for a test that `UpdateAreaOutline` "rejects or accepts a degenerate one- or two-segment outline"; there is no rule to assert, because `Area.SetOutline` (`src/DungeonMaster.Domain/Entities/Areas/AreaMethods.cs:38-44`) does `Clear()` then `AddRange()` with no segment-count check and no null check. **A test cannot assert behaviour that does not exist**, and deciding where the missing invariant belongs -- the aggregate or the validator -- is a design decision outside a coverage session. The aggregate is meanwhile protected only by a caller it does not control: the Application validator rejects empty and null (`src/DungeonMaster.Tests/Application/Features/Dungeons/Validators/UpdateAreaOutlineCommandValidatorTests.cs:89`), so `MASTER-168` is a latent invariant gap rather than a live defect. `MASTER-169` is the same shape -- `Floor..ctor` and `Floor.UpdateNumber` accept any `int` including `0` and negatives (`src/DungeonMaster.Domain/Entities/Floors/Floor.cs:27`), while `Dungeon.AddFloor` and `Dungeon.RemoveFloor` only ever pass `_floors.Count + 1` and `i + 1`, so nothing in the aggregate reaches the unguarded values today and the type still permits them. `MASTER-170` is `src/DungeonMaster.Domain/ValueObjects/PathSegment.cs:18-22` permitting a `CubicBezier` with null control points, contradicting the doc comment on the same type.

**`MASTER-171` is filed on a corrected basis, and the measurement it came from was FALSIFIED during decomposition.** The re-measurement recorded this row as *"`RotateDirection` is dead -- zero references in `src/` outside its declaring file."* **That is false, and it is recorded here as false rather than repeated.** `grep -rn "RotateDirection" src/` returns **0 lines** -- because no identifier by that name exists anywhere in `src/`. `grep -rn "RotationDirection" src/` returns **6 lines in 3 files**: the declaration at `src/DungeonMaster.Domain/Entities/Floors/RotateDirection.cs:3`, which reads `public enum RotationDirection`, and **five live references** at `src/DungeonMaster.App/Views/DungeonDetailPage/ControlsPaletteView.xaml.cs:8`, `:14`, `:15` and `src/DungeonMaster.App/Views/DungeonDetailPage/DungeonDetailPage.xaml.cs:139`, `:150`. Both commands were run in the session worktree at `1407090`, where the declaring folder was still `Entities/Floor/` -- that is what the `RotationDirection` command printed. The declaration path above is written as `Entities/Floors/`, which is where the file stands after sub-session 6b-v pluralised the three entity folders, so the citation stays navigable; it is the current path, not a restatement of that command's output. Neither the file nor the type is renamed by that session, so the finding below is unchanged. The type is **used**; the real and previously unrecorded finding is that the **file name and the type name disagree**, so a search on the file's name -- which is exactly what the measuring agent did -- returns nothing and reads as "unused". Caught by `dir-architect` while decomposing and confirmed by the orchestrator with both commands. This is the same grep-shape failure this whole session was convened to correct in the inherited records, produced one layer further in, inside the session itself, and it is the reason `MASTER-171` is a naming entry rather than a dead-code one.

**`MASTER-172` and `MASTER-173` were found while closing `MASTER-020` and are deliberately not part of it.** The re-measurement recorded both as explicitly not to be folded in, because a closure that quietly absorbs an adjacent gap is how a gap stops being tracked. `MASTER-172` is adjacent to `MASTER-070` and is **not** covered by it: `MASTER-070`'s closure proves token forwarding at the handler and repository boundaries, never at the dispatcher. Neither closure claims either of these.

## Merged groups

- **MASTER-023** (`INFRA-018`, `UI-001`, `XCUT-001`) -- Agree one DbContext and repository lifetime across the Infrastructure and App composition roots
- **MASTER-030** (`UI-021`, `UI-036`, `XCUT-004`) -- Resolve the macCatalyst target framework: implement it fully or drop it from the solution
- **MASTER-048** (`CORE-029`, `XCUT-006`) -- Move the two persistence port interfaces to Application/Common/Interfaces
- **MASTER-132** (`CORE-027`, `UI-029`, `TEST-024`, `XCUT-012`) -- Adopt an .editorconfig fixing tabs as the indent character and reformat the space-indented files
- **MASTER-142** (`UI-034`, `XCUT-015`) -- Settle the app's shipping identity in the Windows manifest and the project file together

Three merges that were considered and rejected, because the test is the fix and not the family:

- **The silent-success trio** (`CORE-001`, `INFRA-012`, `UI-004`) is the closest call in this index
  and does not collapse. The three produce one user-visible behaviour -- an edit that appears to
  succeed and did not -- by three unrelated mechanisms in three assemblies, and no single change
  resolves more than one of them. Giving the Domain mutators return values does not make the view
  model read the `Result` it is already being handed; making the view model read it does not stop a
  mutator no-opping; and neither touches `Attach` marking a detached graph `Unchanged`. They are
  ordered rather than merged: `CORE-001` produces the signal and `UI-004` consumes it, so both sit
  in Session 2 with `INFRA-012`.
- **The lambda-naming and brace records** (`INFRA-007` with `TEST-023`; `UI-028` with `UIKIT-021`)
  each pair cites the same `dotnet-format.md` rule, but the fix is a separate hand edit in a
  separate project with no shared decision behind it. Same rule is not same fix. This is the
  distinction that justifies merging `MASTER-132`, where the four sources genuinely cannot be
  resolved independently: none of them can be fixed at all until the repository decides which
  indent character it uses, and once that one decision is made a single format pass resolves all
  four.
- **`CORE-020` with `UI-017`** -- map geometry with no caller in Application, and map geometry
  trapped in a page -- are the same question but not provably the same fix. Consolidating into
  `Common/Map` resolves both; consolidating into the App project resolves `CORE-020` and leaves
  `UI-017` untestable, because of `MASTER-026`. Since one resolution of the decision fails to close
  both records, they stay separate and share Session 5.

Two notes on effective severity, since the source records are frozen and this index does not
re-litigate them. `MASTER-030` merges `UI-036` (S5, a launch profile listing only Windows) into an
S2 entry, because under the shared macCatalyst decision that observation is not optional -- it is
part of what "implement it fully" means. `MASTER-048` merges `CORE-029` (S4, a file-layout
deviation) into an S3 boundary entry, because the same move that satisfies the layout convention is
the one that gives the persistence port an address.

## Suggested fix sessions

### Session 1 -- Pin the build -- COMPLETE (2026-08-18)
- **Entries:** MASTER-009, MASTER-043, MASTER-071, MASTER-072, MASTER-078, MASTER-104, MASTER-129, MASTER-132, MASTER-140, MASTER-150
- **Combined effort:** ~1.2 days
- **Highest risk:** medium
- **Rationale:** Every entry here changes build configuration and no line of `src/` behaviour, so it is the one session that can land with zero regression risk and it goes first for that reason. It is also enabling work: `MASTER-132` decides the indent character that Session 7 depends on, and `MASTER-078` turns on the enforcement that keeps Session 7 from having to happen again.
- **Outcome:** All ten entries landed. 26 of 26 acceptance criteria met across three tasks, one revision round, no unresolved blocking findings. Build clean at 0 warnings for both App target frameworks under `TreatWarningsAsErrors`; 292 of 292 tests passing. The `no src/ behaviour change` property held -- the only `src/` edits were whitespace, verified by comparing 1,181 string literals byte-for-byte. Session artifacts: `.dir-sdlc/sessions/2026-08-17-pin-the-build/`.

### Session 2 -- Make failure observable in the Application core -- COMPLETE (2026-08-18)
- **Entries:** MASTER-001, MASTER-002, MASTER-003, MASTER-005, MASTER-010, MASTER-016, MASTER-021, MASTER-031, MASTER-045, MASTER-046, MASTER-079, MASTER-080, MASTER-088, MASTER-089, MASTER-090, MASTER-091
- **Combined effort:** ~4.6 days
- **Highest risk:** medium
- **Rationale:** The solution's one S1 sits here alongside the Domain and Application changes that make it meaningful. `MASTER-021` gives the aggregate mutators something to report and `MASTER-001` makes the view model read it; neither is worth doing without the other, which is why they share a session while remaining separate entries. The dispatcher and validation entries ride along because they touch the same files and the same failure path.
- **Outcome:** All sixteen entries landed across nine tasks. **83 of 83 acceptance criteria met**, one revision round, no unresolved blocking findings. Tests **292 -> 434**; build clean at 0 warnings for both App target frameworks under `TreatWarningsAsErrors`, verified on a `--no-incremental` full build. The three silent-success mechanisms are closed by three separate fixes exactly as this index predicted they would have to be: `MASTER-021` gave the Domain mutators a `MutationOutcome` to report, `MASTER-016` replaced `Attach` with `Update` so a detached aggregate persists, and `MASTER-001` made the detail view model surface every `Result` through the `ErrorMessage` channel the other two view models already used. `MASTER-088` was resolved as a genuinely **non-generic `Result` alongside `Result<T>`**, sibling records rather than a hierarchy because a base class cannot be `sealed` and `sealed` is the invariant that entry exists to establish -- which is the answer the five parked design questions (`MASTER-044`, `073`, `074`, `081`, `107`) were waiting on. `MASTER-045` kept the five-member `ErrorCategory` named by `backend-conventions.md` §2 and gave it a production consumer in `ResultMessage`. Two criteria needed a human at the keyboard rather than a test, because `DungeonMaster.Tests` cannot reference the multi-targeted App project (`MASTER-026`, Session 5): both were performed on Windows and observed. Session artifacts: `.dir-sdlc/sessions/2026-08-18-make-failure-observable/`.

### Session 3 -- Settle the data-access lifetime and the persistence contract -- COMPLETE (2026-08-18)
- **Entries:** MASTER-004, MASTER-011, MASTER-012, MASTER-015, MASTER-017, MASTER-023, MASTER-029, MASTER-032, MASTER-033, MASTER-048, MASTER-049, MASTER-050, MASTER-075, MASTER-092, MASTER-093, MASTER-103
- **Combined effort:** ~4.1 days
- **Highest risk:** high
- **Rationale:** One blast radius: `Persistence/`, the two repositories and the two composition roots. `MASTER-023` is the gate -- deciding the `DbContext` lifetime changes what `MASTER-015`'s spurious writes and `MASTER-092`'s transaction boundary even mean -- so it is taken first and the provider-fit findings follow behind it. This session carries the highest risk of the early ones and should not be attempted before Session 2 has stabilised the handlers above it.
- **Outcome:** All sixteen entries landed across three tasks. **48 of 48 acceptance criteria met**, **zero revision rounds**, no unresolved blocking findings. Tests **434 -> 448**; build clean at 0 warnings for both App target frameworks under `TreatWarningsAsErrors`, verified on a `--no-incremental` full build. `MASTER-023` resolved as **`IDbContextFactory<AppDbContext>`, one context per unit of work** -- chosen on performance grounds after a search round, because a root-scoped context accumulates every entity ever loaded and snapshot change tracking compares all of them at every `SaveChanges`. Per-*call* contexts were explicitly rejected: they would leave the aggregate detached at save and send `MASTER-016`'s `Update` down the path that marks the whole graph modified. That decision then settled `MASTER-092` as an explicit `IUnitOfWork.CommitAsync` -- a factory makes the unit of work explicit rather than ambient -- and `SaveChangesAsync` now appears at exactly one production site. `MASTER-029` was taken at the Domain rather than through a converter, and proved smaller than this index recorded: the timestamps are declared **once** on `Entity.cs:16,21` and inherited by all four entity types, so the Domain edit was three lines in one file, not eight properties across four. The stored-format question was settled by running the experiment rather than assuming a format -- the two forms differ only by a six-character `+00:00` suffix, and the reviewer independently confirmed the migrated value is byte-identical to what the `DateTime` model writes natively. Two entries closed partially by design, both declared in the contracts rather than discovered at review: `MASTER-012`'s cold-start half and `MASTER-093`'s startup-refusal and CI halves, the latter because no `.github/workflows` exists to attach to. See `.dir-sdlc/backlog.md`. Session artifacts: `.dir-sdlc/sessions/2026-08-18-settle-the-data-access-lifetime/`.

### Session 4 -- Async, disposal and threading discipline in the presentation layers -- COMPLETE (2026-08-25)
- **Entries:** MASTER-018, MASTER-019, MASTER-024, MASTER-025, MASTER-038, MASTER-039, MASTER-153
- **Combined effort:** ~2.2 days
- **Highest risk:** medium
- **Rationale:** A single discipline applied across three layers: no `async void` outside a guarded handler and no discarded task, plus the layer's `ConfigureAwait` decision. These are individually small, mechanically similar, and each one is a latent crash, so batching them is worth more than spreading them. It comes after Session 3 because a shorter-lived `DbContext` changes what an unobserved task can corrupt. The other half of this rationale as first written -- no undisposed `IDisposable`, no cross-thread field -- is **not** what the session did: `MASTER-006`, `MASTER-013`, `MASTER-055` and `MASTER-062` were rescheduled to Session 6 at Gate 2, so the disposal and threading half is now Session 6's.
- **Outcome:** Seven entries landed across six tasks: `MASTER-018`, `MASTER-019`, `MASTER-024`, `MASTER-025`, `MASTER-038`, `MASTER-039` and `MASTER-153`. Of the **61** acceptance criteria across those six tasks, 59 were met outright, one was met in part and one was not met; both exceptions are manual-observation criteria that no revision round could have closed, and the user accepted each at escalation. Tests **448 -> 469** (`Failed: 0, Passed: 469, Total: 469`), the 21 added being T1's 20 async-discipline guards and T6's one `ConfigureAwait` guard; build clean at 0 warnings for both App target frameworks -- `net10.0-windows10.0.19041.0` and `net10.0-maccatalyst` -- under `TreatWarningsAsErrors`. Three of the seven need qualifying -- `MASTER-019`, `MASTER-025` and `MASTER-039` -- and for two of those, `MASTER-019` and `MASTER-025`, a bare **done** would overstate what was seen. `MASTER-013`, which is one of the four rescheduled rather than one of the seven, needs a fourth. **`MASTER-019`'s four `DungeonListPage` handlers are guarded, and two of them were never seen running**: each opens a `try` above its first `await` and routes the failure to `_viewModel.ErrorMessage`, which rule G1 verifies over the source -- but only `OnCreateClicked` and `OnCardTapped` were observed executing in the running app. `OnEditClicked` and `OnDeleteClicked` never ran once, because the Edit and Delete buttons never materialize on a dungeon-list card: that is `MASTER-159` above, and until it is fixed there is no route to either handler for a human or for a UIA driver. **That qualification is stale as of sub-session 5c (2026-09-01), and is left standing as Session 4's own record rather than rewritten.** `MASTER-159` was found already fixed -- the buttons do render, and 5c's T2 drove both handlers on unmodified code by pointer, and by keyboard once `MASTER-162` was fixed -- so both handlers have now been seen running and `MASTER-019`'s stated reason for doubt no longer holds. **`MASTER-039` is the session's one criterion closed by a human at the keyboard**: an automated `SetCursorPos` / `GetCursorInfo` probe reported ARROW at every point on the changed *and* the unchanged build, including over a native WinUI `TextBox` whose I-beam the platform sets independently of any behavior, which isolated the apparatus rather than the change -- so the reviewer refused "met by baseline parity", and the user then hovered dungeon cards on Windows and reported the hand cursor working across multiple cards without degrading. That observation also settles the review's one unconfirmed risk, that a shared cached `InputSystemCursor` might be closed out from under other elements. **`MASTER-025` landed with one path unobserved**: the blocking file I/O is gone from the `Draw` call path, `LoadOrGetImage` is now a pure cache read and `PreloadObjectImagesAsync` is the only writer, so the artwork observed rendering in place of the `?` placeholder entails the preload ran -- but the **pack-change path was never seen working**, because `src/DungeonMaster.App/Assets/ObjectAssets/ObjectPackRegistry.cs:8-9` declares exactly one object pack, so there is nothing to switch to and re-selecting index 0 raises no event. That path is structurally correct and unexercised until a second pack exists. **`MASTER-013` is not closed, and half of it landed here**: `ClearObjectImageCache()` now disposes each cached image before clearing, purely as a no-regression measure, because the preload changes the cache from holding the kinds a floor happened to draw to holding up to one entry per declared `ObjectKind`, of which there are **37**; the page-teardown half -- `IDisposable`, `Dispose()`, and the call site -- remains Session 6's, and nothing here releases a handle at teardown. **The four rescheduled entries, and why.** `MASTER-006`, `MASTER-013`, `MASTER-055` and `MASTER-062` are three leaks and a memory-visibility race. No test in this repository executes App code -- `DungeonMaster.Tests` targets `net10.0` and cannot reference the multi-targeted App project (`XCUT-002` / `MASTER-026`) -- and none of the four is visible in a running app, so the only evidence available here was a structural assertion over source text, which the user judged insufficient for that defect class. They move to Session 6 because that is where `MASTER-026` lands and they can be demonstrated rather than asserted. Finally, three defects this session found that no audit entry recorded are filed above as `MASTER-159`, `MASTER-160` and `MASTER-161`. Session artifacts: `.dir-sdlc/sessions/2026-08-24-async-disposal-and-threading-discipline/`.

### Session 5 -- Give the editor a seam and the control kit its own contract
- **Entries:** MASTER-007, MASTER-026, MASTER-027, MASTER-030, MASTER-034, MASTER-035, MASTER-036, MASTER-037, MASTER-052, MASTER-053, MASTER-054, MASTER-056, MASTER-057, MASTER-058, MASTER-059, MASTER-060, MASTER-061, MASTER-076, MASTER-077, MASTER-082, MASTER-083, MASTER-084, MASTER-087, MASTER-094, MASTER-095, MASTER-096, MASTER-097, MASTER-098, MASTER-099, MASTER-105, MASTER-106, MASTER-108
- **Combined effort:** ~11.5 days
- **Highest risk:** high
- **Rationale:** The largest and least mechanical session: the editor's missing seam, the control kit's DTO coupling, and the boundary decisions those two force. `MASTER-026` is taken first because it constrains everything else -- where the extracted geometry can live determines whether it is testable at all -- and `MASTER-030`'s macCatalyst decision is here because dropping the target would delete a meaningful fraction of the remaining work.
- **Split into three sub-sessions at Gate 1 (2026-08-26).** At 32 entries and ~11.5 days this is twice the largest session that has run cleanly here, and it spans four independent subsystems, so it was decomposed rather than attempted whole: **5a** the boundary gates (`MASTER-026`, `MASTER-027`, `MASTER-030`, `MASTER-082`, `MASTER-087`), **5b** the editor seam, **5c** the control kit. 5a went first because its two decisions constrain the other two and Session 6.
- **5a outcome -- COMPLETE 2026-08-26.** All five entries landed across four tasks. **67 of 67 acceptance criteria met, zero revision rounds, zero re-dispatches**, three escalations all resolved, five user interactions. Tests **469 -> 506** (`Failed: 0, Passed: 506`, plus a new `Failed: 0, Passed: 1` App suite); solution and both App target frameworks clean at 0 warnings under `TreatWarningsAsErrors`, verified in the main checkout after the merge rather than only in the worktree. Change surface 32 files, +1,867 / -170.

  **`MASTER-026` was resolved as both shapes, not the audit's preferred one.** `XCUT-002` recommended extracting a `net10.0` library and treated multi-targeting the Tests project as the expensive alternative. The cheap shape alone reaches nothing MAUI-typed -- which would have left Session 6's four rescheduled entries (`MASTER-006`, `013`, `055`, `062`) and the user-facing `MASTER-159` with exactly the evidence problem that got them rescheduled out of Session 4. So both were built: `DungeonMaster.App.Core` (`net10.0`, referenced by App and by Tests) **and** `DungeonMaster.App.Tests`, single-targeted to `net10.0-windows10.0.19041.0`. **A test in this repository now executes App code**: `FloorMapDrawableExecutionTests` constructs a real `FloorMapDrawable` and calls `ScreenToCanvas(PointF)`, and the reviewer reproduced its failure under an injected wrong value before restoring it. Multi-targeting that project was measured impossible here -- `NU1201` against `net10.0-maccatalyst26.0`, then an empty `ResolveFrameworkReference.RuntimePackPath` -- and the deviation was approved at Gate 2. **Two limits stand**: the project runs outside a MAUI application host, so `MauiProgram.CreateMauiApp()` is never called, there is no `Application.Current`, and every control deriving from `ContentView`/`ContentPage`/`Shell` is still out of reach -- whether that half is reachable is Session 6's question; and a solution-wide `dotnet test` runs both suites but prints **two per-assembly result lines with no aggregate total**, so a script grepping for one `Failed:` line sees only the first.

  **`MASTER-030` was parked explicitly rather than implemented or dropped**, recorded as `ADR-001` in `docs/architecture-decisions.md`. The decision rests on a fact this index did not have: `net10.0-maccatalyst` **builds clean on this machine** -- `Build succeeded. 0 Warning(s). 0 Error(s).` with the `maccatalyst 26.5.10301` workload installed -- so `XCUT-004`'s claim that nothing here builds the Mac target is false, and `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-05-theme-systemisation.md:22` is stale. What is absent is *runtime*: nothing here can run a macCatalyst build, so "implement it fully" could produce no observed evidence for `UI-021`'s five input paths, and no criterion demanding one was written. The ADR records the target as build-verified and not run-verified, names the five `TODO(macOS)` paths as an accepted non-cosmetic gap, accepts `UI-036` and the missing solution platform unchanged, and states a revisit condition. It also unblocks the `Plugin.Maui.KeyListener` evaluation parked in `.dir-sdlc/backlog.md`, which was waiting on this decision.

  **`MASTER-082` and `MASTER-087` were taken together, and one conflict between two audit records was resolved.** `UIKIT-005` suggested moving the feet-per-tile arithmetic *into* Application beside the map helpers while `CORE-020` moves those helpers *out*; `MASTER-082` won -- the helpers left, and the arithmetic followed them as `TileDistance.InFeet`. `SnapZoneCalculator` and `MapDrawingSession.LastVertex` were **deleted rather than relocated** (a Gate 2 decision: zero production callers each), and `docs/app-application-seam.md` now states what the Application layer's public surface is. Two of this index's own records were falsified in passing: `XCUT-004`'s solution platform list is `Debug|Any CPU` and `Release|Any CPU` only, not `Any CPU`/`x64`/`x86`; and `UIKIT-005` cites the feet arithmetic at `FloorMapDrawable.cs:519-524`, which is at **553-558** after Session 4.

  **One falsified claim moves open work and is deliberately not fixed here.** `UIKIT-022` says `FloorMapDrawable.cs` has "429 space-indented lines and 0 tab-indented lines"; it is tab-indented -- 961 tabs, 459 tab-leading lines, **0** lines starting with four spaces -- and the claim is repeated at `:838`, `:870`, `:1126` and in `MASTER-122`'s row, which carries no done marker. The file-indentation half was almost certainly completed by `MASTER-132` in Session 1 without reconciling `MASTER-122`; the doc-comment half is real and unfixed (36 lines of `///` followed by five spaces). At Gate 3 the user chose to **re-measure the whole 19-file leading-whitespace census before touching the row**, since only this one file was checked. See `.dir-sdlc/backlog.md`. Session artifacts: `.dir-sdlc/sessions/2026-08-26-settle-the-app-boundaries/`.

### Session 6a -- Teardown and lifetime -- COMPLETE (2026-09-02)
- **Entries:** MASTER-006, MASTER-013 (page-teardown half), MASTER-055, MASTER-062, MASTER-160
- **Rationale:** Session 6 was split at Gate 1. 6a took the disposal and threading cluster -- three leaks, a memory-visibility race, and one narrow undisposed path -- because those five share an evidence problem rather than a subject: four of them were rescheduled out of Session 4 for having no evidence stronger than a structural assertion over source text, which the user judged insufficient. 6b keeps the fixture de-duplication and the `App/` sub-suite rebuild, plus `MASTER-167`. `MASTER-161`, `163`, `164`, `165` and `166` were left out of Session 6 entirely at Gate 1: they are sidebar and UI defects, and folding them into a test-suite session is the theme collision that got Session 5 split.
- **Outcome:** **Five entries closed, every one with executed evidence.** Six tasks, six commits, six first-round review passes, zero revision rounds, zero re-dispatches. Tests `640 + 30` -> `640 + 77`; build clean at `0 Warning(s)` for both App target frameworks under `TreatWarningsAsErrors`, verified in the main checkout after the merge, not only in the worktree.

  **The session's premise changed on day one.** A spike established that a full MAUI application host runs in-process in `DungeonMaster.App.Tests` under `dotnet test` -- DI container, `Application.Current`, a `Window` with `AppShell` -- requiring `WindowsAppSdkBootstrapInitialize=true`, a copy of the app PRI to `testhost.pri`, and an STA thread, each proved necessary by removing it and watching the host die. **This is off Microsoft's documented path**, which specifies an `OutputType=Exe` self-hosted app under MTP and states "VSTest doesn't support this unpackaged WinUI configuration"; what was measured here contradicts that note for this configuration. The spike also corrected the standing account of the boundary: the no-host wall is **`Microsoft.Maui.Controls.Element`'s static constructor**, not `ContentView` -- `BoxView` and `Element.AutomationIdProperty` fail identically. The full record is `.dir-sdlc/sessions/2026-09-01-teardown-and-lifetime/spike-host-report.md`.

  **`MASTER-062` is closed as a fix shape, not as a verified race, and the row says so.** `UIKIT-017` records "I have not observed the misfire and cannot state it as fact", and no test can force a memory-visibility reordering. What is demonstrated is that the cross-thread read is gone: `System.Timers.Timer` and the `MainThread.BeginInvokeOnMainThread` hop are removed from both consumers, and the tick now arrives on the thread that started the delay, proven by thread-id equality and by a reviewer swapping the dispatcher timer for a pool timer and watching the ids diverge. Three task reports and code comments at both changed sites state that the race is not demonstrated -- deliberately, so a later reader cannot mistake this for verification. An unrecorded hazard found in passing explains why the `IDispatcherTimer` route was the only viable one: both consumers called `MainThread.BeginInvokeOnMainThread` from inside a `System.Timers.Timer.Elapsed` handler -- a thread-pool thread with no `try` -- so a host-free test of the old code would have **crashed the test host rather than failed**, and the `volatile` alternative would have left that hop in place.

  **`MASTER-055` took the seam route, not the host route, by an explicit Gate 2 decision.** It was assigned to the host fixture at escalation E1; escalation E3 moved it, because the spike never established that `MapCanvas` acquires a real handler and native `FrameworkElement` under the host -- which is precisely the behaviour that entry is about. Its evidence is host-free, over a new `CapturedSubscription<T>` in `App.Core`, and **no criterion, test or comment in the session asserts handler acquisition**; the reviewer verified that boundary held.

  **The session found more defects in its own criteria than in the audit record it was auditing -- five, every one caught by running the check rather than reasoning about it.** Two criteria prescribed an injection that does not discriminate: removing a guard flag left the named test green, because the same code arm also stopped the timer and a stopped fake ignores a tick, so the guard was never reached. That happened twice, in `MASTER-006`'s task and again in `MASTER-062`'s detector half, and both tasks independently wrote re-armed-timer tests to isolate the flag. One criterion named a type that does not exist (`Microsoft.Maui.Controls.Internals.IPanGestureController`; the interface is public at `Microsoft.Maui.Controls.IPanGestureController`). One prescribed an arrangement that cannot produce its own evidence: `page.Handler = null` on a DI-resolved page raises no `OnHandlerChanged` at all, because MAUI's handler setter returns early when assigned the value it already holds -- so `MASTER-013`'s page half would have closed on a test that never triggered teardown, and a reviewer proved the added connect step load-bearing by deleting it against correct code and watching the test fail. One asked for a per-TFM `0 Warning(s)` pair that MSBuild does not print for a multi-TFM build. **All four substantive criteria were amended in the frozen `manifest.json` at Gate 3, each marked inline with the finding and the evidence.**

  **Three tests were found that could pass while asserting nothing**, all the same shape -- an enumeration-based assertion over an empty collection. One shipped in this session (`foreach` over an empty collection, advisory, recorded); two pre-existed in the same session's earlier task (`Assert.All` over an empty `Decoded` list). The general trap is recorded in `.dir-sdlc/backlog.md`.

  **`MauiHostFixture.InvokeAsync` has an implementer and no caller.** Both overloads were kept at Gate 2 as surface provisioned for the page task's asynchronous preload path; that task did not need them. Every task passed every criterion and these two operations are unproven code. See `coverage.md`.

  **One thing the session did to the user's machine and then stopped:** the spike's host boots the real application entry point, which ran the EF migration against the real database at `%LOCALAPPDATA%\DungeonMaster\dungeon-master.db`. `MauiProgram.DatabasePathOverride` now exists so the fixture points at a temporary file, verified by unchanged SHA-256 and mtime across a full suite run. The application never sets that property, and a production call site would be the defect.

  Session artifacts: `.dir-sdlc/sessions/2026-09-01-teardown-and-lifetime/`.

### Session 6b -- Rebuild the App sub-suite and de-duplicate the fixtures
- **Entries:** MASTER-008, MASTER-014, MASTER-020, MASTER-028, MASTER-040, MASTER-041, MASTER-042, MASTER-063, MASTER-064, MASTER-065, MASTER-066, MASTER-067, MASTER-068, MASTER-069, MASTER-070, MASTER-085, MASTER-086, MASTER-100, MASTER-101, MASTER-102, MASTER-109, MASTER-127, MASTER-138, MASTER-149, MASTER-155, plus MASTER-167 assigned here at Session 6's Gate 1 -- **26 entries** — the four on this list that Session 6a closed (`MASTER-006`, `013`, `055`, `062`) removed, `MASTER-167` added; `MASTER-160`, 6a's fifth closure, was never on this list
- **Combined effort:** ~6.6 days
- **Highest risk:** high
- **Rationale:** The whole test suite in one pass, after the code it tests has settled. Sequencing matters more here than anywhere: rebuilding the `App/` sub-suite before Session 5 has decided where App logic lives would rebuild it against a shape that is about to change. `MASTER-065` and `MASTER-014` are taken together because the shared fixture and the `EnsureCreated` schema are the same four files. It also now carries `MASTER-006`, `MASTER-013`, `MASTER-055` and `MASTER-062`, rescheduled here from Session 4 at Gate 2 -- see the Session 4 outcome above for why, and note that half of `MASTER-013` already landed there.

### Session 6b-i -- Rebuild the App sub-suite -- COMPLETE (2026-09-08)
- **Entries:** MASTER-008, MASTER-028, MASTER-040, MASTER-063, MASTER-068, MASTER-100, MASTER-101, MASTER-102, MASTER-109, MASTER-138, MASTER-149 -- **11 of Session 6b's 26**, chosen at Gate 1 as the ones concerning the `App/` test sub-suite. The remaining 15 belong to 6b-ii (fixtures and de-duplication, 10) and 6b-iii (coverage gaps and tooling, 5).
- **Outcome:** **Eleven entries closed, plus one accepted addition.** Twelve tasks, twelve commits, **fourteen reviews and fourteen first-round passes, zero blocking findings, zero revision rounds**, one re-dispatch after an API session limit killed a reviewer. 146 acceptance criteria, 146 met. Tests `640 + 77` -> `695 + 77 + 23`, plus a new out-of-solution end-to-end project; solution build clean at `0 Warning(s)` under `TreatWarningsAsErrors`, and **every session-touched `.cs` file confirmed CRLF in the main checkout after the merge**, not only in the worktree. Change surface 45 files, +9,069 / -1,755, range `83ba80c..4133a68`, merged `ce4113d`.

  **The session's foundation was chosen by measurement, not by the audit's suggestion.** Research established that Microsoft documents no supported way to unit-test XAML or views out of process -- the answers are view models, or Appium -- which reframed `MASTER-028` and `MASTER-109`: asserting over the declaration is defensible, and the regex implementation was the defect. A spike then proved Microsoft's documented plain-`net10.0` path works here, so `DungeonMaster.App` gained a third, platform-less target framework and a new `DungeonMaster.App.UnitTests` project reaches real types, real `BindableProperty` metadata and the real merged theme dictionaries **with no MAUI host and no WinUI**. `DungeonMaster.Tests` deliberately keeps no reference to the app, because acquiring one imports 18 MAUI global usings and produced 31 `CS0104` errors when measured.

  **Two entries close FULLY that were expected to close partially, and both upgrades came from running a check.** `MASTER-028`: the rationale for leaving `GeometryScaleTests` and `ThemeHygieneTests` on raw text was that an XML model could not see literals inside attribute values -- an injection over ten synthetic cases found the opposite, that the two forms disagree on exactly one case and it goes the XML model's way (`#FF&#x30;000`, which no byte-level pattern sees). `MASTER-063`: the `RegexOptions.Compiled` survey found **six** method-body construction sites, two of them absent from this entry's own `locations` list.

  **Three of this index's own records were corrected by measurement.** `TEST-004`'s claim that "an attribute value containing `>` truncates the tag" holds only for a **literal** `>`, and only when it precedes the attribute being sought -- an `&gt;` entity contains no `>` character and the old regex handled it. `TEST-016` names 7 helper-assertion sites; there are **14**, and its citation for `GeometryScaleTests.cs` pointed at a `[Fact]`-level assertion that was correct where it stood while the real helper assertion sat 100 lines away. `TEST-015`'s premise is confirmed and quantified: all three of the icon font's encoding records alias one format-4 subtable, so formats 0, 6 and 12 were unreachable from any test.

  **`MASTER-040`'s mechanism is corrected here rather than left standing.** The `char` cast truncated `U+1F6E1` to `U+F6E1`, a **length-1** private-use string, which the old `text.Length != 1` test **accepts** -- so the old scan reached that control and reported it under a codepoint it does not have. It was **misreported, not dropped**. The delivered fix and its injection are sound; the account everyone had of the defect was wrong, including this index's, the criteria's, and the commit message's.

  **`MASTER-149` closes without being made to fail, deliberately.** The ratchet iterates an allow list that is empty and asserts the empty result is empty. That is correct -- it is a self-arming ratchet, needed at the moment somebody adds a first entry, which is the moment nobody would think to write it. An `Assert.NotEmpty` would make a test that cannot *pass*. The remedy is visibility: it now carries a display name stating it is deliberately inert, and a paired experiment demonstrated the ratchet works -- armed with a real offender it passes **in 15 ms against its inert `< 1 ms`**, and with the offender removed it fails naming the stale entry.

  **The strongest single result is about method rather than code.** Across three tasks, blinding a scan left **every fact built on it green** -- ten facts in one case, four in another, and in a third the fact that `continue`s past a missing declaration. Only the newly promoted guards failed. **An assertion inside a helper protects nothing: a scan reaching nothing passes exactly like a scan reaching everything.** That is `MASTER-102` measured rather than argued.

  **One accepted addition, scoped in at Gate 2 by the user's decision:** the repository had **no end-to-end layer**. `src/DungeonMaster.App.UiTests` now launches the real unpackaged executable through Appium, clicks a control by automation id, and asserts a different element's text -- shown able to fail with the click removed while both control facts stay green in the same red run. It is deliberately **not** in `src/DungeonMaster.sln`, because its tests cannot pass without a running Appium server. **It applies pending EF migrations to the user's real database on every run** -- `MauiProgram.DatabasePathOverride` is an in-process static with no out-of-process seam -- which is carried in the backlog.

  **Seventeen defective citations, counts and prescribed commands were found in this session's own frozen artifacts, every one by running a check and none by reading.** Two were the orchestrator's. The recurring one is a leading-tab ERE grep that returns `0` on a fully tab-indented file: it appeared five times, twice inside frozen criteria, and the second occurrence was authored *after* the form had been adjudicated and the measurement sent to its author -- in a task whose criterion 10 forbids the construct that its criterion 2 mandates. A manifest lint is carried in the backlog, because prose has demonstrably not stopped it.

  **Six contract operations shipped with an implementer and no consumer** -- `ThemeFileText` has zero call sites anywhere; `ThemeDirectory` and `StaticResourceKeys` are used only inside their own file; `KeyDefinitionPattern`, `LegacyTolerantKeyDefinitionPattern` and `ThemeTokenNotFoundException` are reached only from their declaring task's own tests. Three were surfaced independently by three different tasks before the Gate 3 search ran. Carried to the backlog as **one** decision, since narrowing any of them is a contract change.

  **The Session 6 summary row above was corrected by this session.** It read "Not started" while Session 6a had already landed five entries on 2026-09-02. Marking another session's *entry rows* is not this session's to do, and none were touched -- but a session-level row asserting "Not started" against sixteen landed entries is a falsehood in the index, so it now reflects 6a and 6b-i together.

### Session 6b-ii -- Fixtures and de-duplication -- COMPLETE (2026-09-08)
- **Entries:** MASTER-014, MASTER-041, MASTER-042, MASTER-064, MASTER-065, MASTER-066, MASTER-067, MASTER-069, MASTER-085, MASTER-127 -- **10 of Session 6b's 26**, chosen at Gate 1 as the fixture and de-duplication set. The remaining 5 belong to 6b-iii (coverage gaps and tooling).
- **Outcome:** **Ten entries closed. Eight by code, two with no code change at all.** Five implementation tasks, five commits, five reviews, **five first-round passes, zero blocking findings, zero revision rounds**; 66 acceptance criteria across those five tasks, 62 met and 4 not met -- and all four were adjudicated by the reviewers as defects in the criteria rather than in the work, three of them demanding a whole-project test count from a worktree that five concurrent tasks were writing to. Commits `71972eb`, `a36074d`, `8b74f54`, `cf32061`, `8784326` on `dir/2026-09-08-fixtures-and-de-duplication`, off `850a10b`.

  **`MASTER-066` and `MASTER-041` closed against a commit that predates the session, and one of them would have been actively harmful to work.** Both were remediated by `bcb49dc` (2026-08-18), an ancestor of HEAD. `TEST-010` directs the deletion of four `Received(1).UpdateAsync(...)` assertions; `UpdateAsync` no longer exists anywhere (`git grep -c "UpdateAsync" <rev> -- 'src/*.cs'` returns 45 at `bcb49dc^` and 0 at `850a10b`), and the four assertions standing at those sites today are `DidNotReceive().Update(...)` -- they pin the fix, not the defect. Injecting the pre-fix behaviour put 14 tests red, every one on the mock assertion alone, with a passing control that failed a different 6. **Following the entry as written would have deleted the only thing in the suite that catches save-on-failure.** That is the case for re-measuring an entry before acting on it, and it is stronger than any count below.

  **`docs/audit/05-tests.md` is systematically stale -- and the cause is drift, not a careless auditor.** The document was authored at `2603170` on **2026-08-17** (`git log --diff-filter=A -- docs/audit/05-tests.md`). **28 commits touched `src/DungeonMaster.Tests` between `bcb49dc^` and `850a10b`** (`git log --oneline bcb49dc^..850a10b -- src/DungeonMaster.Tests | wc -l`; the same command against this session's HEAD returns 33). That is the mechanism. This session started out describing the audit as "systematically under-counting", and **that framing was measured and is false**: on every count re-checked against the day it was written, the audit named every file that then existed. Recording the distinction matters in both directions -- an entry excused as drift when it was a counting error teaches the next reader to trust the wrong thing, and convicting a careful auditor of sloppiness they did not commit is the same failure the other way.

  **The mis-citations, each re-measured against `2603170` as well as `850a10b`.** Seven were carried into this session as defects. **Measured, five are real and two are not.**

  | # | Entry | Claim | Measured | Class |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | 1 | TEST-002, TEST-009, TEST-026 | four Infrastructure classes | `git grep -c "EnsureCreated" <rev>` returns **4** at `2603170` -- exactly the four each lists -- and **7** at `850a10b`. The three missed classes were created 2026-08-18, the **day after** the audit (`git log --diff-filter=A -- <path>`) | **DRIFT** |
  | 2 | TEST-002 | "the two migrations" | `git ls-tree -r --name-only <rev> -- src/DungeonMaster.Infrastructure/Migrations/` returns **2** at `2603170` and **3** at `850a10b`; the third is `UtcDateTimeTimestampsAndScaleWithoutStoreDefault`, dated 2026-08-18 | **DRIFT** |
  | 3 | TEST-026 | said to under-count against itself, body "four" against five `locations` | **NOT A DEFECT.** Its five locations span **four** files -- two point into `AreaPersistenceDiagnosticTests.cs` -- so the list and the body agree, and 4 was true then | **no defect** |
  | 4 | TEST-009 | "the same nineteen lines exist four times" | **NOT A DEFECT.** Its own cited span `DungeonReadRepositoryTests.cs:12-30` is exactly **19 lines** at `2603170` and ends at `CreateContext()`, which the entry's body names as part of the block. The "seventeen" this session carried measures a narrower span stopping at `Dispose()` | **no defect** |
  | 5 | TEST-008 | CreateDungeon and DeleteDungeon have no not-found test | `git grep -n "NotFound" <rev> -- '.../DeleteDungeonCommandHandlerTests.cs'` returns **nothing** at `2603170` and **two hits** at `850a10b`, added by `bcb49dc`. CreateDungeon still has none | **DRIFT** |
  | 6 | TEST-013 | five snap-zone facts; three uniform geometry facts; three geometry line ranges | The snap-zone files existed at `2603170` and were deleted 2026-08-26 in `1678d12`; `NoBareMinimumSizeValues` was added 2026-08-28 in `64c4253`, making the true figure four. **But the title says "Five geometry facts" while the body names three** -- self-contradictory on the day | **DRIFT**, except the title, which is a **MEASUREMENT ERROR** |
  | 7 | TEST-012 | two subsumed pairs | Only one holds. `UpdateArea_SecondRenamePersistsAcrossReload` reloads and overwrites the name in a second unit of work, so it never observes the first rename's persistence -- shown by injection with a passing control. Both tests stand at `2603170` as they do at `850a10b`, so this was wrong when written. **The prose is right about the other pair**: it names `WithEmptyOutline` as the wider test, and this session's own artifact was wrong to say otherwise | **MEASUREMENT ERROR** (reasoning) |

  **So: four drift, two authorship errors, and two claimed defects that measurement did not support.** The two real authorship errors are both of a kind no repository movement could cause -- an entry contradicting itself, and an entry reasoning wrongly about code it quotes correctly.

  **Two of this session's own governing figures were wrong and are corrected here rather than left to propagate**, both found by running a command rather than by reading: the directed `nineteen` to `seventeen` swap for `TEST-009`, refused because the two numbers measure different spans; and the claim that `TEST-026` under-counts against itself, which its own `locations` list does not support.

  **`docs/audit/05-tests.md` was corrected in place by this session**, entry by entry, each correction carrying its command, its anchor commit and its drift-or-error classification, with the previous wording kept verbatim rather than overwritten. **That is a change of plan made at Gate 2**: this record was originally to report the stale entry bodies as an open gap for a later session, and the user scoped the correction in instead.

  **What 6b-iii must do before it decomposes anything.** It inherits five TEST-\* entries -- **`MASTER-020`, `MASTER-070`, `MASTER-086`, `MASTER-155` and `MASTER-167`** -- and **none of them was re-measured by this session**. Whether they are equally stale is **suspected, not known**. Every one of the ten entries above needed re-measuring before it could be acted on, one of them would have caused a regression if taken at its word, and the corrections landing in `docs/audit/05-tests.md` (`e55eb85`) cover **six records only** -- TEST-002, TEST-008, TEST-009, TEST-012, TEST-013 and TEST-026, carrying seven numbered corrections plus one cross-reference in the layer verdict; the other 26 records in that document are unchecked, not confirmed. **Re-measure each of the five against a fixed commit first, and decompose from the measurement, never from the entry's prose.**


### Session 7 -- Style, naming and documentation sweep
- **Entries:** MASTER-022, MASTER-110, MASTER-111, MASTER-112, MASTER-113, MASTER-114, MASTER-115, MASTER-116, MASTER-117, MASTER-118, MASTER-119, MASTER-120, MASTER-121, MASTER-122, MASTER-123, MASTER-124, MASTER-125, MASTER-126, MASTER-128, MASTER-130, MASTER-131, MASTER-133, MASTER-134, MASTER-135, MASTER-136, MASTER-137, MASTER-139, MASTER-141, MASTER-142, MASTER-143, MASTER-144
- **Combined effort:** ~3.3 days
- **Highest risk:** medium
- **Rationale:** Pure style, naming and documentation, every entry `independent: true`, and it goes last for two reasons: reformatting files that six earlier sessions are still rewriting wastes the work, and by this point Session 1's `.editorconfig` and `EnforceCodeStyleInBuild` mean most of it can be verified by the build rather than by a reviewer.

Seventeen `S3` and `S5` entries are deliberately unassigned:

- **Five design questions that should be re-asked after Session 2 lands, not before**: `MASTER-044`
  (`Result<T>.Value` nullability), `MASTER-073` (`ObjectKind` has no zero value), `MASTER-074`
  (entity identity equality), `MASTER-081` (no policy layer) and `MASTER-107` (the `Dungeon`
  delegation facade). Each describes a shape that the `Result`-flow work in Session 2 will either
  justify or contradict -- `MASTER-088`, which is assigned, redesigns `Result<T>` itself, and the
  answer to that changes what these five should become. Deciding them now risks doing the work twice.
- **Two isolated cleanups with no natural companion**: `MASTER-047` (five Domain overloads reachable
  only from tests -- their fate depends on `MASTER-086`'s coverage work) and `MASTER-051` (the
  duplicated Entry/Editor handler mappers, which `MASTER-030` may delete outright if macCatalyst
  goes).
- **Ten S5 observations** -- `MASTER-145`, `MASTER-146`, `MASTER-147`, `MASTER-148`, `MASTER-151`,
  `MASTER-152`, `MASTER-154`, `MASTER-156`, `MASTER-157`, `MASTER-158`. By definition these deviate
  from no authority. `MASTER-157` in particular is an affirmation, not a defect: it records that the
  hand-rolled dispatcher earns its keep. They are listed so a maintainer can act on them, not
  scheduled.

The nine entries added after the audit are also unassigned, and each carries a suggested home rather than a scheduled one. Scheduling them is a decision for whoever plans the next session. `MASTER-162` is the exception: sub-session 5c both filed and closed it, and it is described here for the record rather than for scheduling.

- **`MASTER-159` -- `DmCard`'s trailing content never renders, so the Edit and Delete buttons are unreachable on every dungeon-list card.** `DmCard.OnHoverEntered` (`src/DungeonMaster.App/Controls/DmCard/DmCard.xaml.cs:101-105`) sets `TrailingPresenter.IsVisible = true`, but that presenter is a bare `ContentView` (`DmCard.xaml:52-55`) whose `Content` is only ever assigned from the `TrailingContent` property-changed callback at `DmCard.xaml.cs:46` -- which does not run for the `TrailingContent` set inside the list's `DataTemplate` (`DungeonListPage.xaml:49-141`), so the presenter renders empty. Hover fires -- the card lightens and `PointerOver` applies -- but neither button appears, in a 19-descendant UIA tree or in a full-window screenshot. **This is user-facing: a user cannot edit or delete a dungeon from the list.** Found by Session 4's T5 while trying to satisfy an observation criterion, and pre-existing -- `Controls/DmCard/` is unmodified against the session's base commit. Rated `S2` rather than `S1` because it removes two affordances from one control's trailing region and corrupts nothing; this index's sole `S1`, `MASTER-001`, was a silent failure on every write path of a view model. Suggested home: **Session 5**, which owns the control kit. **This description is falsified as of sub-session 5c (2026-09-01), and is left standing as Session 4's own record rather than rewritten.** 5c's T2 reproduced the entry on **unmodified** code at `fda46b7`: hovering a dungeon-list card grew the UIA tree from 27 to 30 elements and surfaced `EditDungeonButton`, `StartSessionButton` and `DeleteDungeonButton`, all with `IsOffscreen=False`, and both handlers were then driven -- Edit navigating to the Dungeon Editor, Delete raising the confirmation popup. The stated mechanism is separately falsified: the project sets `MauiXamlInflator=SourceGen`, so the `DataTemplate` body is compiled C#, and `Views_DungeonListPage_DungeonListPage.xaml.xsg.cs:848` contains `dmCard.SetValue(global::DungeonMaster.App.Controls.DmCard.TrailingContentProperty, horizontalStackLayout);` inside the factory lambda -- verified independently by the orchestrator. Session 4's observation is attributable to a DPI-broken pointer probe that never reached the card: T2 reproduced that exact false symptom with a DPI-unaware probe and isolated it with an apparatus control on an unrelated `PointerGestureRecognizer`, the same class of apparatus failure this index already records for `MASTER-039` above. The entry closed as **done** (5c) -- found already fixed, with no code changed for it.
- **`MASTER-160` -- a narrow undisposed path inside `PreloadObjectImagesAsync`.** If `stream.Dispose()` throws after `src/DungeonMaster.App/Graphics/FloorMapDrawable.cs:76` has already assigned a decoded image, control reaches line 84 and overwrites that non-null entry with `null` without disposing it. This is conformant with the contract Session 4 froze -- a per-entry failure stores `null` -- and the window is vanishingly small, so it is not a defect in what that session built; it is simply real, and nothing else in the file has this shape. Fix shape: decode into a local, close the stream, then assign. Suggested home: **Session 6**, alongside `MASTER-013`, whose teardown disposal touches the same cache.
- **`MASTER-161` -- `FloorSidebarItem`'s card body is tappable but offers no cursor affordance.** The `VerticalStackLayout` filling the control's root `Border` carries `TapGestureRecognizer Tapped="OnCardTapped"` (`src/DungeonMaster.App/Controls/FloorSidebarItem/FloorSidebarItem.xaml:33-35`) and expanding the floor is the only route to its areas, but no `HandCursorBehavior` is attached to it; the control's three declarations of that behavior are all on child `Button`s -- notebook (`:86`), delete (`:106`) and add-area (`:158`) -- and the root `Border`'s own recognisers (`:164-172`) are pointer and drop only. The analogous `DmCard` self-attaches the behavior at `DmCard.xaml.cs:93`, so the two card types disagree. A clickable surface that does not look clickable. Suggested home: **Session 5**, with the rest of the control kit.

  **How this block and correction 2 were WRONG on their first pass, recorded deliberately and not quietly fixed.** Both originally glossed the superseded lines from memory of two narrow `sed` windows rather than from one read of the span: this block said `:93` "is a closing brace followed by a doc comment" -- true of `:91`-then-`:93`, but not of `:93` -- and correction 2 said `:108` "is inside a comment block", which described `:112-115` and not `:108` at all. **The load-bearing corrections were right both times**; only the asides about what the old citations now land on were wrong. **This was the eighth instance in sub-session 6b-iv of a claim about a file being restated instead of re-derived, and it happened inside the amendment written to eliminate that pattern**, in the task whose contract section exists solely to bind its author against it. **That is the finding worth keeping: prose warnings against this pattern have now failed eight times in one session, and on every one of those eight occasions the thing that caught it was somebody running the command.** A reader who takes one rule from this index should take that one.**]**
- **`MASTER-162` -- `DmCard`'s trailing Edit and Delete buttons were unreachable by keyboard, because the reveal was gated on the pointer alone.** `TrailingPresenter` was revealed only from `OnHoverEntered`, so a keyboard-only user could focus the buttons but never see them, and in practice never reached them. Found by sub-session 5c's T2 while reproducing `MASTER-159`, and deliberately **not** folded into that entry's status: `MASTER-159` was found already fixed, and closing this defect inside that row would have credited the session with a fix it did not make and buried a real accessibility defect in a closed row. **Fixed in 5c** under escalation E2: hiding is now by `Opacity` rather than `IsVisible`, `TrackTrailingFocus` subscribes `Focused`/`Unfocused` on every descendant of the assigned content, and `UpdateTrailingReveal()` reveals when `_pointerOver || _focusedDescendants > 0`; `InputTransparent` is gated on the reveal state so the permanently laid-out region does not eat the card's own tap. Two residues were accepted rather than closed, and belong to whoever revisits this control: the hidden buttons stay UIA-exposed with `IsOffscreen=False`, so a screen-reader user in scan mode meets six action buttons a sighted user sees no hint of; and `StartSessionButton` is now a permanent tab stop on a floored dungeon, so a keyboard user can activate the unwired no-op recorded as `MASTER-154`. Rated `S2` on `MASTER-159`'s own reasoning -- two affordances of one control's trailing region, corrupting nothing -- narrowed to one input modality rather than all users.
- **`MASTER-163` -- the floor drag handle sits under WinUI's overlay scrollbar, so floor reordering cannot be performed at all.** The handle is `Grid.Column="4"`, the last column, hard against the card's right edge (`src/DungeonMaster.App/Controls/FloorSidebarItem/FloorSidebarItem.xaml:164-171`), and the floor list sits inside the `ScrollView` at `src/DungeonMaster.App/Views/DungeonDetailPage/DungeonDetailPage.xaml:667`. WinUI draws overlay scrollbars over content at exactly that edge, so whenever the list is long enough to scroll -- which is the feature's normal case, reordering a single floor being meaningless -- the scrollbar covers the only affordance for reordering. User-observed 2026-09-01. **Pre-existing**: sub-session 5c changed neither the column layout nor the `ScrollView`. It also explains, retroactively, why 5c's synthetic drag probe failed identically on the unchanged baseline; the apparatus was not the only problem, the target is genuinely unreachable. Rated `S2` because a shipped feature is unusable in its normal case for every user, a larger population than `MASTER-159`'s two affordances, and short of `S1` because nothing is corrupted and no other path is affected. Fix shape: move the handle out of the scrollbar gutter, or reserve that gutter in the row's grid. Suggested home: **Session 6**, with the rest of the sidebar's reachability work.

- **`MASTER-166` -- the optimistic ghost frame `AddGhostArea` exists to paint was never observed reaching the screen.** After the closing click that completes an outline, sub-session 5c's T7 captured a 45-frame burst at 60 ms: 41 frames pixel-identical to each other over ~2.6 s, then a frame pixel-identical to the post-reload frame. The intermediate frame -- the outline shown before the database write returns, which is the whole reason the ghost mechanism exists -- was never captured. **Pre-existing, unchanged by 5c, and not investigated**: a negative capture is not proof that the frame never renders, only that a burst at this rate never caught one, so the mechanism is unconfirmed and any entry acting on this should begin by establishing it. Rated `S3` because if it holds the cost is perceived latency on one interaction rather than a wrong result, and because the finding is not yet confirmed.   **[AMENDED 6b-iv, 2026-09-11 -- a MEASUREMENT of the write leg was taken, AND IT IS NOT A PROVEN CAUSE of the 45-frame negative capture. Classification: MEASUREMENT ERROR, in that this entry recorded no measurement where one was available.**  Sub-session 6b-iv's `T4` instrumented the persistence leg against a **file-backed** SQLite database at production settings, n = 100 after 10 discarded warm-ups:

  | | min | median | p90 | max | over 16.7 ms |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | file-backed | 7.26 ms | **8.93 ms** | 11.50 ms | 16.62 ms | **0 / 100** |
  | in-memory (control) | 3.86 ms | 4.15 ms | 5.63 ms | 7.27 ms | 0 / 100 |

  **FOUR CAVEATS TRAVEL WITH THAT `0/100` AND IT MUST NOT BE CITED WITHOUT THEM.** They are recorded verbatim on the preserved instrument at `.dir-sdlc/sessions/2026-09-11-make-the-floor-sidebar-operable/artifacts/OptimisticGhostLatencyProbe.cs.txt`, which also carries the command to re-run it.

  1. **The max sat 0.08 ms under the threshold** -- 0.5%, well inside run-to-run noise. A re-run on the same machine could plausibly report a non-zero count, and **that would confirm rather than contradict this finding**: it would show the distribution's tail touching the frame boundary, which is what these numbers already say.
  2. **`16.7 ms` is one frame at 60 Hz, ASSUMED and not measured.** The threshold is hardcoded in the probe. On a 120 Hz display it halves to 8.3 ms -- **below this distribution's own median of 8.93 ms** -- so the `0/100` does not survive the change and more than half these samples would cross.
  3. **The probe exercises the handlers directly and excludes `CommandDispatcher`**, so validation and pipeline overhead are not counted. **The number is a FLOOR on real latency, not a ceiling.**
  4. **Steady state was one dungeon, one floor, zero areas** -- the cheap end of the realistic range, since a floor carrying many areas makes the reload leg more expensive. The **file-to-memory ratio of ~2.2x at the median** is the more portable result than either absolute number, and it is why an in-memory harness would have been useless here.

  **THIS IS A MEASUREMENT, NOT A CAUSE.** It does **not** explain sub-session 5c's negative 45-frame capture and must not be recorded as having done so. This entry already concedes the governing point in its own text -- *"a negative capture is not proof that the frame never renders"* -- and the converse holds just as strongly: a write leg that fits inside a frame budget is not proof that the ghost frame rendered. What the measurement narrows is the hypothesis space, nothing more. **Status is left UNCLOSED and carries no commit hash**: `T6` cannot invoke git, and the orchestrator writes the closure with commit evidence at Gate 3.**]**
- **`MASTER-167` -- `GreaterThanZeroConverter` reports an unresolved binding as a positive count whenever the `Invert` parameter is used.** `Convert` computes `bool result = value is int n && n > 0;` (`src/DungeonMaster.App/Converters/GreaterThanZeroConverter.cs:38`), which is correctly `false` for `BindableProperty.UnsetValue` -- the sentinel MAUI supplies when a binding path does not resolve -- and then negates it at `:42` when `parameter` is `"Invert"`. An inverted binding on an unresolved path therefore returns `true` and **shows** the element it was written to hide. **Two live consumers**, both measured 2026-09-01: `src/DungeonMaster.App/Views/DungeonDetailPage/DungeonDetailPage.xaml:151` and `src/DungeonMaster.App/Views/DungeonListPage/DungeonListPage.xaml:115`, the latter the transparent `BoxView` overlay carrying "Add at least one floor before starting a session" -- so a typo or a rename in either bound path puts that tooltip permanently over an enabled Start Session button, which is the inverse of what it is there to say. Found by sub-session 5c's T1 as advisory A1 while fixing `MASTER-007`, and **not fixed**: 5c changed nothing in this converter. Rated `S2` on `MASTER-007`'s own reasoning, and deliberately the same rating -- it is the same defect class, the same `UnsetValue` sentinel rendered as a positive assertion about the data, surviving in the converter `UIKIT-004` declares immune. **`UIKIT-004` is not falsified by this and must not be recorded as such.** Its written claim is that this converter's bare `value is int n` test rejects the sentinel (`docs/audit/04-app-controls-and-theme.md:225-226`), which is true and which 5c's T1 verified against an unmodified `Convert`; the defect is in the `"Invert"` negation applied afterwards, which that record does not discuss. T1's reviewer ruled it explicitly **not** a contradiction for exactly that reason. Fix shape: test the sentinel explicitly before applying `Invert`, as `MASTER-007` did in `NotNullConverter`. Suggested home: **Session 6**, with the rest of the converter work.

  **[AMENDED 6b-iii, 2026-09-11 -- CORRECTION 2, the stated trigger is wrong for these two sites, and the stated consequence does not follow from it. Classification: MEASUREMENT ERROR.**  Previous wording, verbatim: *"so a typo or a rename in either bound path puts that tooltip permanently over an enabled Start Session button, which is the inverse of what it is there to say."* Both pages declare a root `x:DataType` (`DungeonDetailPage.xaml:10`, `DungeonListPage.xaml:10`) and the App project sets `MauiXamlInflator=SourceGen` (`src/DungeonMaster.App/DungeonMaster.App.csproj:45`), so these bindings are compiled: **a typo or a rename in either bound path is a BUILD FAILURE, not a runtime sentinel.** The stated user-visible consequence therefore does not follow for these two sites as they stand. What the defect actually is at these two sites is a **latent trap**, whose realistic triggers are a change to the bound property's *type* or a future consumer written without `x:DataType` -- neither of which the build catches. The finding is real and the fix was right; the causal story attached to it was not. Recorded because a reader who tests the entry's stated trigger will find it does not reproduce and may conclude the entry is wrong altogether.**]**

- **`MASTER-174` -- a floor drop that misses every drop target is discarded SILENTLY, with no error and no feedback.** `FloorListContainer` (`DungeonDetailPage.xaml:668`) carries no `DropGestureRecognizer`, so a release between two cards, or on any space inside the list that no handler owns, reaches nothing at all: the drag ends, the card returns, and the application says nothing. There is no error line, no toast and no log entry -- the operation simply does not happen, and a user who was aiming at a gap has no way to tell a missed drop from a broken feature. **Filed as the silent discard, and deliberately NOT as "the reorder does not reorder".** That second characterisation is what this session believed it had found, and it is **known false**: filing it would have put a fresh false claim into the very index this session was convened to correct. **The reorder feature WORKS, end to end.** Observed with the user on 2026-09-11, maximized at 1920x1080 with all 11 floors of Test Dungeon #1 visible and **no overlay scrollbar rendered at all** -- which isolates the run from `MASTER-163` entirely: the source card vanished at drag start (so `OnDragStarting` fired), the target card showed the gold `DragOver` stroke, a placeholder gap appeared, the order changed as expected, and no `Could not reorder the floors` line appeared. **The database confirmed the move persisted** -- `Guest Rooms` from floor 3 to floor 7 -- though only on the second read: the first was taken from a `.db` snapshot copied **without its live `-wal`**, reported to the user as "completely unchanged", and caught afterwards. Transcript at `.dir-sdlc/sessions/2026-09-11-make-the-floor-sidebar-operable/observations/T1-reorder.md`. **None of `T1`'s four enumerated root-cause candidates is the mechanism**, and that is not a failure of the investigation: under the observed conditions **there is no defect in the reorder path for a mechanism to explain**. The discriminator table eliminated two candidates outright and the remaining two both predict *no reorder* -- but the reorder happened, correctly. **`T1` DID NOT FIX A REORDER DEFECT and must not be recorded as having done so.** It delivered a behaviour-preserving refactor and the first tests ever to exist on the gesture-to-command path, and it **correctly declined to ship a speculative fix** for a defect it could not reproduce -- which is the right call and should read as one. This entry is `MASTER-163`'s **symptom**, not a peer defect; it carries its own id so the silent-discard behaviour can be cited and closed independently. A remedy shipped against it in 6b-iv -- a drop on the seam between two cards now reorders, landing on the upper card, confirmed by a human observer with the sidebar scrolling at 900x640 -- but **no status and no commit hash are written here**, because `T6` cannot invoke git; the orchestrator writes the closure with commit evidence at Gate 3. The `DungeonDetailPage.xaml:668` citation is sourced to this session's `measurement.md` and **qualified "at `b3b1641`"**: that file was edited by `T1`-`T4` and `T6` did not re-verify the line. Rated `S2` on `MASTER-163`'s own reasoning -- a shipped feature fails in its normal case for every user -- and short of `S1` because nothing is corrupted. Suggested home: with `MASTER-163`, which it is a symptom of.

- **`MASTER-175` -- `AreaSidebarItem`'s card body carries no `HandCursorBehavior` at all.** **FOUND AND FIXED in sub-session 6b-iv; this is not an open item.** The root `Border x:Name="ItemBorder"` carries a `TapGestureRecognizer Tapped="OnItemTapped"`, so the row body is a clickable surface and the largest one in an expanded floor -- but it had no `HandCursorBehavior` attached, while the two `Button`s nested inside it did. The pointer therefore went hand, arrow, hand as it crossed a single row. **This is ABSENCE, and it is not a duplicate of `MASTER-165`:** that entry alleges **inertness** -- a behavior attached that fails to take effect -- and is **falsified**; this one says the behavior was never attached. Only this one reproduces, and it is the surviving half of the 2026-09-01 observation that produced both. **What shipped:** `T3` added a single `<behaviors:HandCursorBehavior />` inside the existing `Border.Behaviors` block on that root `Border`, matching the attachment already present on the floor card body. **Nothing else changed** -- no size, no padding, no margin, no gesture, no visual state -- `AutomationId="AreaRow"` was preserved, and the two existing `Button` attachments were retained. Confirmed by a human observer hovering an area row's body (not its notebook or delete buttons) in the running application: **the hand cursor appeared.** Recorded at `.dir-sdlc/sessions/2026-09-11-make-the-floor-sidebar-operable/observations/T3-sidebar.md`, step 2. **Verified by opening the file** in the 6b-iv worktree: the root `Border` and its `x:Name` are at `AreaSidebarItem.xaml:12-13`, `AutomationId="AreaRow"` at `:14`, the `TapGestureRecognizer` at `:138`, the two `Button` attachments at `:111` and `:131`, and the new `Border.Behaviors` block at `:150-155` with `HoverOrLongPressBehavior` at `:151-153` and `HandCursorBehavior` at `:154`. **A caveat on that last range, stated rather than smoothed over:** this session's contract lists `AreaSidebarItem.xaml` among the files no task changed and cites the `HoverOrLongPressBehavior` at `:141-145`. `T3` **did** change this file, and the behaviors block has since moved to `:150-155` -- the line numbers above are what the worktree contains now, read directly from it, not the pre-fix ones. **No status and no commit hash are written here**, per this session's correction/closure split: `T6` cannot invoke git, and the orchestrator writes the closure with commit evidence at Gate 3. Rated `S3` to match `MASTER-161`, its own family -- a cursor affordance is missing and nothing is unreachable.

- **`MASTER-176` -- the floor drag handle has no keyboard route AT ALL, so floor reordering is pointer-only.** `OnKeyDown` (`DungeonDetailPage.xaml.cs:361-430`) declares no reorder key, and the `DragGestureRecognizer` on the handle is the only reorder affordance in the source -- both `ReorderFloorCommand` call sites sit inside drop handlers. A keyboard-only user cannot reorder floors by any route. This is not a missing shortcut to an otherwise reachable operation; there is no non-pointer path to the operation at all, which the frontend discipline this session composed forbids: every interactive element must be reachable **and operable** by keyboard alone. **THE SIGNIFICANCE, and it is the reason this is filed rather than noted:** `MASTER-164` restored keyboard access to a floor's **areas** in this same session, which means **reorder is now the remaining pointer-only operation in the sidebar.** Before 6b-iv it was one inaccessible thing among several and easy to defer as part of a larger problem; it is now the last one, and the argument for deferring it again is correspondingly weaker. **`T3` RAISED THIS AS AN ADVISORY AND CORRECTLY DID NOT ACT ON IT.** That is a scope decision, not neglect, and a reader must not conclude a task let it slip: `T3`'s task was layout-only, and this session's contract `floor-reorder.md` §3 **freezes the gesture host**, so adding a keyboard route would have meant editing a frozen surface mid-session while parallel tasks depended on it. The right move was to file it, which is this entry. **Filed OPEN. No task in sub-session 6b-iv implements it, no status is written and no commit hash appears** -- and unlike the other entries in this group, that is not the correction/closure split at work: there is genuinely nothing to close. Escalation E9. The `OnKeyDown` citation is sourced to this session's `measurement.md` §1 and **qualified "at `b3b1641`"**: `DungeonDetailPage.xaml.cs` was edited by `T1`-`T4`, `T6` cannot invoke git, and it did **not** re-verify that range. Rated `S2` on `MASTER-164`'s own reasoning -- a blocked navigation path rather than a missing semantic label -- with effort `S` and risk `medium` as this session's judgment, not a measured value. Suggested home: with `MASTER-163` and `MASTER-174`, the rest of the sidebar's reorder work.
